<image-desc>A circular bookplate (ex-libris) belonging to Alfred Mauger. The border contains the capitalized text "EX LIBRIS" at the top and "ALFRED MAUGER" at the bottom. In the center are large initials "AM". Flanking the initials are the Greek letters "ΓΝΩ" on the left and "ΣΙΣ" on the right (forming the word ΓΝΩΣΙΣ, or Gnosis). Above the initials is a small tau cross, and below them is an Egyptian ankh next to another symbol. At the very bottom, outside the circle, is the engraver's mark "Stern. Paris".</image-desc>

E fa ogni membro albato,
Riſolue é ben liquora

E vietto é retto

E nulla ſenſa ſale
Pratica noſtra vale:

## ALTRO VERSI.

L’arte ſta in aque pura
E altro à far non cura
Genera la tintura
Coſa che al foco dura;
Mercurio ſtruger ſuole
Ogni foliato ſole
Lo diſſolue é fa el mole
L’alma del corpo il tole
E dopo lo congela
A chi Dio lo riue la.

# STANCES SVR LA FIGVRE
## ſuiuante du Phœnix.

->I.<-

*Dieu qui tout compoſa du plus pur de la terre,*
*Quand ce Chaos fut fait, & ce qui luy enſerre,*
*Il le mit au pouuoir de toute la Nature,*
*Qui nous fait veoir au iour le Sel, Soulfre & Mercure.*

->II.<-

*Ce pourpris eſtant fait, & cette maſſe ronde,*
*Les Elemens vnis & tout ce qu'eſt au monde,*
*Les Germes qui y ſont, ce qui eſt en Nature,*
*Ils naiſſent par le Sel, le Soulfre & le Mercure.*

->III.<-

*Par eux tout le pouuoir ſe met en euidence,*
*L'eſtre qui s'en enſuit d'vne meſme prudence.*
*Tant que continuant de iour en iour Nature,*
*Fait agir ſous le Ciel le Sel, Soulfre, & Mercure.*

->IIII.<-

*Plus tout ſe continuë d'vne grand fermeté,*
*Plus tout ce qui ſe faict eſt ſolide arreſté,*
*Et par Indiuidus ſe diſpoſe Nature,*
*Par leſquels ſe refait le Sel, Soulfre, & Mercure.*

->V.<-

*Puis encore touſiours elle ſe multiplie,*
*Faiſant qu'en alterant la terre ſoit remplie*
*D'humeur, que tous les ans en la riche Nature,*
*L'eſprit ſe recompoſe en Sel, Soulfre, & Mercure.*

<vocab>Phœnix, Nature, Sel, Soulfre, Mercure, Chaos, Elemens, Alchimie</vocab>

<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>illustration</page-type>
<meta>Alchemical emblem engraving, likely from the 17th century. The page contains no primary text, only symbolic imagery and faint bleed-through from the reverse side.</meta>

<image-desc>
An intricate alchemical engraving featuring several symbolic levels:

At the top, a naked, bearded man wearing a crown (representing Saturn or Chronos) stands atop the apex of a large triangle. He holds a scythe in his right hand and a pair of dividers or a compass in his left. To his left are seven falling drops (representing mercury, dew, or tears), and to his right is a large, swirling mass of flame.

Inside the central triangle, a phoenix is depicted rising from flames, its wings spread wide. A cross symbol is attached to the bottom of this triangle.

The middle register features three celestial/planetary symbols. In the center is a large symbol for Mercury. To the left is a crescent moon with a human face (Luna), and to the right is a radiant sun with a human face (Sol).

The bottom register shows a landscape or mound representing the earth. Centered on the mound is a solid cube (symbolizing the Philosopher's Stone, salt, or the element of Earth). The cube is flanked by various flowering plants, including roses on the right and lilies or carnations on the left. The sky in the background is rendered with dense horizontal hatching.
</image-desc>

<vocab>Alchemy, Saturn, Phoenix, Mercury, Sol, Luna, Cube, Hermeticism, Transmutation</vocab>

A circular alchemical woodcut emblem. Around the circular border is the Latin phrase "Visita Interiora Terræ Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem" (forming the acronym VITRIOL). 

Inside the circle:
- At the top center is a chalice or goblet.
- To the left of the chalice are symbols for the Sun and Mars (♂).
- To the right are symbols for the Moon and Venus (♀).
- Below the chalice is the symbol for Saturn (♄) inside a dark orb, and to its right, the symbol for Jupiter (♃). The chalice stem is formed by the symbol for Mercury (☿).
- Two hands emerge from clouds on the left and right sides of the circle, pointing inward.
- At the bottom, three shields are linked by a central ring. The left shield depicts a lion rampant. The right shield depicts a winged creature (likely a phoenix or eagle). The bottom shield contains an eight-pointed star.
- Next to the lion shield is a textured orb (possibly the moon). Next to the phoenix shield is a globe marked with grid lines.
- In the very center, between the shields, is a globus cruciger (an orb topped with a cross).
</image-desc>

A detailed alchemical engraving, framed by a single line border. The central scene depicts a "Chemical Wedding" or symbolic union. In the middle, a King and Queen join their right hands, presided over by a figure in ecclesiastical robes and a mitre, resembling a Bishop or High Priest. The Queen (left) wears a crown and a dress with a ruffled collar; above her in the clouds is a crescent moon. The King (right) wears a crown, a fur-lined mantle, and holds a scepter; above him in the clouds is a personified sun with a face.

On the far left, an alchemist or laboratory assistant in simple attire tends to a brick furnace (athanor) topped with a conical cap. On the far right, a large metallic or ceramic vessel sits directly atop a blazing wood fire; three faces (or a three-headed being) emerge from the steam or flames at the top of the vessel. In the background, between the King and the boiling vessel, a white swan is visible. The engraving is signed at the bottom right, just above the border: "J. Gobille. fe".
</image-desc>

A faint, mirrored bleed-through of an alchemical engraving from the reverse side of the leaf. It depicts the personification of Justice (Iustitia) with her scales and sword, positioned behind a large circular emblem. Within the circle is a square containing a triangle. Inside the triangle is the mirrored word "EVN" (likely "EIN" or Greek "ΕΝ" for "One"). Surrounding the triangle are mirrored German and Latin words: "Natur" (top), "Prudentia" and "Patientia" (sides), and the phrase "Das ist die Philosophia" (bottom). Various alchemical and planetary symbols, including a crescent moon at the top and a sun at the bottom, are arranged around the outer ring of the circle.
</image-desc>

## CLEF. VII.

---

<image-desc>
An alchemical engraving set within a square frame. A female allegorical figure stands behind a large circular emblem. She holds a sword in her right hand and a pair of scales in her left, symbols typically associated with Justice. Behind her head is a cylindrical object labeled "Sceau Hermetiq" (Hermetic Seal).

The central circular emblem contains several layers of symbolic text and geometry:
- The outermost ring features the letters **C**, **H**, **A**, **O**, **S** (arranged as C at the top, H at the right, A at the bottom-right, O at the bottom-left, and S at the top-left).
- Inside the circle is a square. Each side of the square is labeled with a season: "Hyuer" (Winter) at the top, "Printemps" (Spring) on the right, "Este" (Summer) at the bottom, and "Automne" (Autumn) on the left.
- Within the square is a triangle containing the word "EAV" (Water).
- At the base of the triangle/square is the text "Sel des Philosophes" (Salt of the Philosophers).

The artist's signature "J. Gobille. fe." is located in the bottom-right corner of the illustration's border.
</image-desc>

<vocab>Clef, Sceau Hermetiq, Hyuer, Printemps, Este, Automne, EAV, Sel des Philosophes, J. Gobille, Chaos</vocab>

<header>CLEF. VIII.</header>

<image-desc>An emblematic engraving representing the "Eighth Key" of an alchemical series (traditionally attributed to Basil Valentine). The scene is set in a landscape bounded by a distant arched wall. In the upper center, a target on a pedestal is pierced by an arrow; a large key hangs suspended from a cord above it. Two archers sit on barrels or stools to the left and right, aiming crossbows at the central target. 

In the middle ground, a man emerges from an open grave with his hands raised in prayer. To the left, a figure in a long gown stands holding a staff. The field is marked with several small crosses. In the foreground, a skeleton lies prostrate on the ploughed soil. To the right, a man in a tunic and cap is sowing seed across the furrows. A group of birds is gathered at the far right edge. The engraver's signature is visible in the bottom right corner of the frame.</image-desc>

J. Gobille . fe

# NEVFIESME CLEF
## de l'œuvre des Philosophes.

### CHAPITRE IX.

SATVRNE le plus haut des Pla-
nettes, est le plus bas & abiect en
nostre magistere, il tient neãtmoins
la principale Clef, & estant le vil, &
n'ayant presque point d'authorité, il
tient le plus beau lieu ; & bien que
par sa volonté il se soit acquis le plus
haut par dessus les plus hautes Pla-
nettes, il doit toutesfois cheoir au
plus bas, en luy couppant les aisles,
& estre sa lumiere obscure, grande-
ment diminuée, & par sa mort ve-
nir toute la perfection de l'œuvre,

<vocab>Saturne, Clef, magistere, Planettes, Philosophes, perfection de l'œuvre</vocab>

An alchemical engraving contained within a square frame. Two nude figures are joined in an acrobatic pose: a female figure on the right stands on a small radiating star or sun, bent forward with her hair hanging down; a male figure on the left is positioned horizontally with his legs raised, supporting a black bird (likely a raven or crow) on his feet. A bird with wings spread is near the male's face, and a winged serpent or small dragon is near the female's head. Below them is a circle containing three hearts arranged in a triangular pattern, intertwined with three serpents.

- In the bottom apex: the planetary symbol for Mercury (☿) above the Hebrew string כרדכ.

A rectangular engraving depicting an alchemical allegory. In the foreground, two lions are locked in a struggle, biting each other's mouths. Each lion is being ridden by a woman with long, flowing hair. The woman on the left holds up a heart-shaped vessel from which sprout two crescent moons flanking a central sun with a human face. The woman on the right holds a similar vessel from which sprout a sun with a face and a single crescent moon. Behind the woman on the right, a knight in full plate armor stands on the lion's haunches, holding a sword raised above his head. In the lower-left corner, a small dog or lion cub is depicted. The background shows a wide landscape featuring a fortified town with towers and spires set against a range of mountains. The engraving is signed in the bottom right corner: "J. Gobille. fe."

# CLEF. XII.

<image-desc>
A detailed alchemical engraving from Basil Valentine's "Twelve Keys". In the center, a man dressed in 17th-century attire (a doublet with buttons, voluminous breeches, and a conical hat) stands in a laboratory holding a pair of long tongs. To the right, a large barrel-shaped furnace (athanor) with iron hoops and a small arched opening at the base emits thick, swirling clouds of smoke and bright flames. 

In the background, a workbench holds various items: apothecary jars on a high shelf, books, a brush, and a plant with two large flowers. Between the two flowers is the alchemical symbol for Mercury. A window above the bench reveals a crescent moon on the left and a radiant sun with a human face on the right. 

In the lower-left foreground, a lion stands over a serpent, which appears to be entering or emerging from the lion's mouth. The engraver's signature is visible on the floorboards near the bottom.
</image-desc>

->J. Gobille. f.<-

# AZOTH,

## OU LE MOYEN DE FAIRE l'Or caché des Philosophes.

### *De Frere Basile Valentin.*

<image-desc>A symbolic alchemical woodcut. In the center is a "Philosophical Tree" growing from a small patch of earth. The tree has five main branches at the top, each ending in a many-pointed star containing a planetary/metallic symbol: Venus (♀), Mercury (☿) at the highest point, Saturn (♄), Mars (♂), and Jupiter (♃). A large equilateral triangle points upwards, enclosing the upper branches. Below, two figures stand on either side of the trunk: on the left, an older bearded man ("Senior") in a long robe and wide-brimmed hat holding a staff; on the right, a younger man ("Adolphus") in 17th-century aristocratic dress with a sword and a feathered hat. The older man points toward a symbol of Earth/Antimony (♁) while the younger points toward a square (□) within a downward-pointing triangle that connects the two figures. To the left of the tree is a personified, radiating Sun (Sol), and to the right is a personified crescent Moon (Luna).</image-desc>

->A PARIS.<-

->Chez **Pierre Moet**, Libraire Juré, proche le Pont S. Michel à l'Image S. Alexis.<-

->M. DC. LIX.<-

<warning>The text on this page consists of a mirrored offset (ink transfer) from the facing title page. The primary intended content of this page is the alchemical woodcut illustration.</warning>

<image-desc>
A detailed alchemical woodcut illustration known as the "Arbor Philosophica" or Philosophical Tree. A central tree with a robust trunk grows from a small mound. Its branches spread symmetrically to the left and right. Superimposed on the upper half of the tree is a large equilateral triangle. At the very top of the central branch is a large seven-pointed star containing a human face, representing the Sun. Six smaller seven-pointed stars (representing the other planetary metals) are placed on the outer branches, three on each side. To the left of the tree, a man dressed as a pilgrim or wanderer is depicted with a wide-brimmed hat, a staff in his hand, and a pack on his back. Above the pilgrim's head is a winged or flaming disk. To the right of the tree, a shrouded or seated figure is visible in the background. The entire scene is enclosed within a thin rectangular border.
</image-desc>

The outer border contains the Latin text: "Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem" (Visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying you will find the hidden stone). Inside the circle, two hands emerge from clouds on the left and right. At the top center is a radiant sun with a face, flanked by a crescent moon. Alchemical symbols for the planets are arranged in the upper half: Mars and Saturn on the left, Venus and Jupiter on the right, with Mercury depicted within a central chalice. At the bottom, three shields are linked by chains. The left shield depicts an eagle (or phoenix), the central shield shows a seven-pointed star beneath a globus cruciger, and the right shield depicts a lion. Two globes (celestial and terrestrial) are placed between the shields.</image-desc>

## Declaration & explication d' Adolphe.

<image-desc>A detailed woodcut alchemical emblem known as the VITRIOL diagram. The central feature is a large circle containing a seven-pointed star (heptagram) emanating from a central human face. Each point of the star is associated with a number (1-7) and a planetary symbol. Around the circumference of the circle, Latin text reads: "Visita Interiora Terra Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem." 

Inside the spaces between the star's points are circular medallions containing symbolic scenes:
1. A bird or phoenix.
2. A unicorn.
3. A bird of prey.
4. Two birds (pelican in her piety).
5. A tree with birds.
6. A figure emerging from water.
7. A lion.

Outside the main circle are three labels: "ANIMA" (left, next to a personified Sun), "SPIRITVS" (right, next to a bird and a personified Moon), and "CORPVS" (bottom center, between two legs standing on land and sea). The entire diagram is framed in a square border with decorative elements in the corners, including a moon goddess/Diana figure on the lower right.</image-desc>

Et ceux dont l'elixier par l'art se doit former?
Pour des corps imparfaits les deffauts reformer,
Sont en estre, en substance, & vertus uniforme
Pareils en qualitez, mais diferens en formes.
Nature les prepare; & en les preparant;
Elle rend à nos yeux leur aspect different.

Au centre de la terre elle tient sa boutique,
Ou d'engin admirable elle assemble & fabrique
Des principes premiers ces principes prochains;
Dont elle va formant de ses expertes mains
Vne masse confuse, ou par poids elle assemble,
Les quatre qualitez des deux spermes ensemble.

Ayant meslé l'eau seiche avec l'esprit puiant,
Sa fournaise elle enflamme; & les va transmuāt
En substance fumeuse, ou vapeur qui sans cesse
Monte si quelque obstacle opposé ne l'abaisse.

Si rien ne la reprime à force de voller
Elle eschappe fuitiue; & va former en l'air
Quelque instrumēt du foudre: ou l'aspect fatidique
D'vne errante Comethe, & feu Metheorique.

Mais trouuant vn rempart qu'elle ne perce pas,
Elle est reverberée, & recourbée en bas.
Puis s'escartant pressée, aux plus estroites veines

--- Page 400 ---
<language>French</language>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<page-num>194</page-num>
<header>S T A N C E S.</header>

<margin>Vive eau
Sous l'espec
de l'esprit, et d'air</margin>
Vif eau qui brille, & roulle aussi claire qu’argēt;
Espece esclatante, humide, & inconstante
D’un air epuré de ceste mer flottante.

<margin>Comme apres
l'ame & l'esp.
Corps. & C.</margin>
Comme apres que la terre aura son eau repris,
L’ame & l’esprit seront dessou us les corps compris;
C’est la terre ou il faut que l’or meure & pour-
risse,
Comme le sperme humain en l’humaine matrice.

On void les vegetaux par la terre produits,
Par <term>putrefaction</term> estre en terre reduits:
Terre qui en vertu la premiere surpasse,
Par son <term>sel</term> qui l’anime, & qui la rend plus grasse.

Ceux qui du labourage ont pratiqué le train,
Ont en soin de la paille aussi bien que du grain:
Car la paille pourrie en graisse conuertie,
Se rejoint à la terre, & luy donne la vie:

Dont apres, son grain propre, en un tel champ
semé,
Est plus abondamment produit & animé.

Les metaux, du <term>Mercure</term>, ont tiré leur semence:
Il est leur propre terre & luy seul à puissance
De les reduire en luy par <term>putrefaction</term>,
Pour donner aux parfaicts plus de perfection.

Car nos corps submergez dans les flots du Mer-
cure,
Et transmuez en luy par propre pourriture:
Sont la terre feconde, & les champs fructueux,
Ou nos beaux grains semez se font plus vertueux.

->F I N.<-

<vocab>Vif eau, Mercure, putrefaction, sel, vegetaux, sperme humain, alchimie, transmutation</vocab>
